What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Well, I'm here because lately I just... I can't seem to keep my head clear at work. I'll be sitting there and I just completely lose track of what my clients are saying to me. And then with the paperwork, I catch myself rereading the same chart note over and over again without it actually sinking in. \n\nIt's getting really frustrating because I just kind of freeze up on really simple decisions that I used to handle without a second thought. So, yeah, I figured I needed to come in and talk to someone about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling sad or down lately?\n\nPatient: Um, not really, no. I mean, I wouldn't say I feel sad. I haven't really noticed feeling down or anything like that. It's mostly just... just the frustration with my head not working right when I'm trying to do my job.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling anxious or on edge lately?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I'm not sitting there feeling nervous or panicky or anything like that. \n\nIt's really just the focus issues. I get a little annoyed with myself when I can't process what I'm looking at, but I wouldn't say I've been feeling anxious or on edge.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been experiencing these focus issues?\n\nPatient: It's been about a month now. It started right around the time our clinic lost a few staff members. My schedule got shifted around, so now I'm doing a lot of late-evening coverage, and I've had to take on a heavier load of crisis paperwork. That's when I really started noticing the issues.\n\nDoctor: Do you ever feel empty or uninterested in things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, actually, I have. My guitar has just been sitting in the corner lately. I keep bailing on soccer and volunteering at the shelter. And even when I try to force myself to do things I usually like, like cooking or going hiking, it just feels like I'm going through the motions. It's like there's no point to it.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you're just going through the motions most of the day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, exactly. Like I was just saying, it really does feel like that pretty much the whole day. Even when I try to do my normal stuff—like cooking or hiking, or picking up my guitar—it's all just going through the motions. I just do what I have to do, but it doesn't feel like there's any real point to any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've been having a low mood lately?\n\nPatient: I'm not really sure. I haven't noticed feeling sad or down, if that's what you mean by a low mood. I've been skipping meals, but not really on purpose... it's just that most days, I don't even feel hungry until someone reminds me that I need to eat. It just feels like I'm going through the motions with no point to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. I mean, I'm not sitting there feeling nervous or panicky or anything like that. \n\nIt's really just the focus issues. Like I was just saying, it really does feel like that pretty much the whole day. I think that's why my guitar is just sitting in the corner, and why I keep bailing on soccer and the shelter. I've been skipping meals, but not really on purpose... it's just that most days, I don't even feel hungry until someone reminds me that I need to eat. So I guess I've lost some weight because of that.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel pleasure when you're doing things that you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Like I mentioned a minute ago... no, I really don't. There's just no pleasure in any of it anymore. That's why my guitar has just been sitting in the corner, and why I keep bailing on my soccer games and volunteering at the shelter. Even when I try to force myself to do something I used to like, um, like cooking or going hiking, I don't feel anything good. It just feels like I'm going through the motions with no point to it.\n\nDoctor: Have you been having thoughts of death or suicide?\n\nPatient: I don't have a plan to hurt myself or anything like that. But, um... I do keep having these thoughts, like... like maybe it would just be easier if I didn't wake up in the morning. And honestly, that scares me, because being a therapist, I know exactly what that can mean.\n\nDoctor: Have you been experiencing these thoughts of not waking up in the morning daily?\n\nPatient: Um, no, I wouldn't say it's every single day. It's... I haven't really kept track of the exact days, but it's not a daily thing. Just, you know, often enough that it bothers me when it happens.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling uninterested in things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for about a month now. Um, it started right around the time the clinic lost some staff. My schedule got shifted into late-evening coverage, and I've had to take on a lot heavier crisis paperwork. So, yeah, it's been about a month of just... not caring about my usual stuff.\n\nDoctor: Would you say you've lost a significant amount of weight, like more than 5% of your body weight, over the past month?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually weighed myself to know if it's exactly five percent, but... yeah, I've definitely lost some weight. My pants have gotten pretty loose lately. It's just that most days I don't even feel hungry until someone actually reminds me to eat, so I've been skipping meals without really meaning to.\n\nDoctor: Have you experienced a loss of interest in activities for most of the day, nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Yeah, pretty much all day, most days. Like, my guitar has just been sitting in the corner gathering dust. I keep bailing on my weekly soccer games and my shifts volunteering at the animal shelter. And even when I try to do stuff I usually like, like cooking a nice meal or going for a hike, it just feels like I'm going through the motions. There's just no point to it, you know? I just don't get anything out of it anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Um, not really, no. I wouldn't say I feel sad. I'm not really walking around crying or feeling upset or anything like that. It's more just... well, like I mentioned, just that feeling of not caring about much. But I haven't really noticed feeling sad during the day.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel sad most of the day?\n\nPatient: Uh, I think I just mentioned this, but no, not really. I wouldn't say I feel sad most of the day. I'm not crying or feeling down like that. It's really just that flat feeling I was talking about, where I just don't care about much.\n\nDoctor: Do you think the feeling of not caring about things is present even on the days when you don't have those thoughts about not waking up in the morning?\n\nPatient: Uh, wait, I don't actually have thoughts about not waking up in the morning. I'm not really sure where that came from. I don't have thoughts like that at all. It's really just the, um, the not caring about things that I deal with every day. But yeah, I'm not wishing I wouldn't wake up or anything like that.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing.
